  The Domain & Use Cases: We need a robust data foundation to evaluate 5 core dynamics:

  1. Yield & Weather Dynamics (correlating actual generation with local weather)
  2. Market Cannibalization Effects (impact of high supply on wholesale prices)
  3. Infrastructure Tracking (distribution of installed capacity)
  4. Predictive AI (forecasting generation based on weather and capacity)
  5. Seasonal Dynamics (profiling generation over hours and months)

  (LOCF) to clean, impute, and join the data into a Master Time Spine on CAS.
  2. Modeling: Use PROC R to fit Generalized Additive Models (GAMs) that predict energy generation based on weather, capacity, and seasonal indices. Ensure seamless bidirectional data exchange using sd2df
  and df2sd so that data never leaves the governed SAS platform.
  3. Reporting: Create a SAS script that generates an ODS HTML report containing specific visual blueprints for each of the 5 key use cases to provide actionable insights to business users.

  Documentation Requirements:

  1. Write and maintain both a German (Readme.md) and English (Readme_en.md) documentation file.

[6233bae5-4fd2-46ba-b70e-0a5354c6b72c]
<USER_REQUEST>
2. Der Extraktions-Prozess (Local SQLite Parsing)
Die historischen Token-Daten der letzten Wochen existieren, sie werden von Google jedoch nur lokal auf Ihrer Maschine geloggt und nicht über das CLI-Frontend exponiert. Um diese auszuwerten, müssen Sie die Rohdaten selbst verarbeiten.

Gehen Sie wie folgt vor:

Lokalisieren Sie die SQLite-Datenbanken: Navigieren Sie zu ~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/conversations/. Jede Konversation der letzten Wochen ist dort als separate .db Datei gespeichert.

Identifizieren Sie die Tabellen: Die Metriken liegen nicht als flache Integer-Felder vor. Sie müssen die Tabellen gen_metadata und steps auslesen.

Deserialisieren Sie die Protobuf-BLOBs: Die genaue Token-Abrechnung (unterteilt in input, output, cache_read, cache_write und thinking_output) ist innerhalb von Protobuf-BLOBs in diesen Tabellen serialisiert. Ein simpler SQL-Dump über die Shell reicht hier nicht aus, da die Binärdaten decodiert werden müssen.

Automatisieren Sie die Aggregation: Der effizienteste Weg für dieses Datenformat ist die Kombination aus Python und DuckDB. Schreiben Sie ein Skript, das iterativ alle .db-Dateien im Verzeichnis per DuckDB einliest, die Protobuf-Strukturen über eine Python-UDF deserialisiert und die Token-Werte mit einem GROUP BY über die Zeitstempel der letzten Wochen aggregiert.
</USER_REQUEST>

<properties>
<property name="type"><![CDATA[pub]]></property>
<property name="interval"><![CDATA[3 seconds]]></property>
<property name="code"><![CDATA[import esp

from kafka import KafkaConsumer, TopicPartition
 
TOPIC = "@KAFKA_TOPIC@"

PARTITION = int(@KAFKA_PARTITION@)
 
consumer = KafkaConsumer(

    bootstrap_servers="@KAFKABOOTSTRAPSERVERPORT@",

    enable_auto_commit=False,

    auto_offset_reset="earliest",

    value_deserializer=lambda value: value.decode("utf-8", errors="replace"),

)
 
topic_partition = TopicPartition(TOPIC, PARTITION)

consumer.assign([topic_partition])

consumer.seek_to_beginning(topic_partition)
 
poll_count = 0
 
def publish():

    global poll_count

    poll_count += 1
 
    try:

        records = consumer.poll(timeout_ms=2000, max_records=100)

        events = []
 
        for _, messages in records.items():

            for message in messages:

                events.append(

                    {

                        "event_id": message.offset,

                        "msg_body": message.value,

                    }

                )
 
        esp.logMessage(

            logcontext="python_kafka_consumer",

            message=(

                f"poll={poll_count}; "

                f"assignment={list(consumer.assignment())}; "

                f"records={len(events)}"

            ),

            level="info",

        )
 
        return {

            "events": events,

            "done": False,

        }
 
    except Exception as error:

        esp.logMessage(

            logcontext="python_kafka_consumer",

            message=f"Kafka poll failed: {error!r}",

            level="error",

        )

        return {

            "events": [],

            "done": False,

        }]]></property>
</properties>
</connector>
</connectors>

https://go.documentation.sas.com/doc/en/espcdc/v_075/espca/p0sbfix2ql9xpln1l1x4t9017aql.htm

/science:opentargets-database is a [Slash Command]:
<SKILL>The user has explicitly invoked the (science:opentargets-database) skill. You must strictly follow the instructions in this skill to process their request. Here are its contents:
# Open Targets Database Skill

## Overview

This skill provides access to the Open Targets Platform GraphQL API. It
aggregates multi-modal evidence from genetics (GWAS/eQTL), pathways, animal
models, and clinical trials to rank target-disease associations and identify
druggable genes.

## Prerequisites

1.  **`uv`**: Read the `uv` skill and follow its Setup instructions to ensure
    `uv` is installed and on PATH.
2.  **User Notification**: If .licenses/opentargets_database_LICENSE.txt does
    not already exist in the workspace root directory then (1) prominently
    notify the user to check the terms at
    https://platform-docs.opentargets.org/licence, then (2) create the file
    recording the notification text and timestamp.

## Core Rules

-   **Use the Wrapper**: ALWAYS execute the provided helper scripts to query the
    database rather than accessing the database directly. The scripts
    automatically enforce fair use and implement retry logic.
-   **Output Flag**: The `--output` flag is always required as output can be
    very large. Use `jq` or write your own code to process this JSON file.
-   **Notification**: If this skill is used, ensure this is mentioned in the
    output.

## Quick Reference

Always use the provided Python script `scripts/query_opentargets.py` to quickly
query the database. It handles API communication, retries, formatting, and
automatically truncates overly large responses. NEVER write your own curl or
similar requests.

**Usage:**

```bash
uv run scripts/query_opentargets.py --output /tmp/opentargets_results.json [OPTIONS] COMMAND [ARGS]...
```

**Common Options:**

-   `--output PATH`: **Required**. Path to write the JSON output file.
-   `--limit N`: Limit the number of items returned in arrays (default is 50).
    Use a smaller number like 10 when doing preliminary exploration.
-   `--page-size N`: Set the API pagination size (default is 200). Increase if
    you need more results (e.g., a study with many credible sets).

**Available Commands:**

-   **`get-gwas-studies`** *`disease_id`*: Fetches all GWAS studies associated
    with a specific disease ID (e.g. `MONDO_0008383` for Rheumatoid Arthritis).
-   **`get-study-credible-sets`** *`study_id`*: Fetches all credible sets for a
    given study ID (e.g. `FINNGEN_R12_RX_CROHN_2NDLINE`). Returns confidence,
    finemapping method, variant, and p-value info.
-   **`get-qtl-credible-sets`** *`variant_id`*: Retrieves QTL credible sets for
    a specific variant ID (e.g. `19_44908822_C_T`).
-   **`get-l2g`** *`variant_id [--study-id ID]`*: Returns Locus-to-Gene (L2G)
    predictions/scores for a locus to identify the most likely causal gene. Only
    `variant_id` is required; use `--study-id` to filter to a specific study.
    Accepts `chr` prefix (e.g. `chr1_113834946_A_G`).
-   **`get-target-druggability`** *`ensembl_id`*: Provides tractability data
    (small molecule, antibody, etc.) and clinical trial safety info for a
    gene/target.
-   **`get-associated-targets`** *`disease_id`*: Find all target genes
    associated with a specific disease ID (EFO or MONDO).
-   **`get-disease-drugs`** *`disease_id [--min-stage STAGE]`*: Find all drugs
    and clinical candidates associated with a disease. Use `--min-stage` to
    filter (e.g., `PHASE_3` for Phase III or Approved).
-   **`get-associated-diseases`** *`ensembl_id`*: Find all diseases associated
    with a specific target Ensembl ID.
-   **`search-disease`** *`query_string`*: Search for a disease by name to find
    its ID and other metadata.
-   **`get-credible-sets-near-target`** *`ensembl_id [--window N]`*: Fetches
    credible sets for a target and filters them to those within a genomic window
    around the target. Useful for finding variants "nearby" a gene.
-   **`custom-query`** *`query [--variables '{}']`*: Run a raw GraphQL query for
    any other Open Targets data.

## L2G Query Usage

The `get-l2g` command has two modes:

*   **Variant only** (`get-l2g <variant_id>`): Returns L2G predictions from
    **all credible sets across all studies** where that variant is the lead
    variant. This can return a large number of results (e.g., hundreds). Use
    this when the user wants a broad view of which gene is most likely causal at
    a locus, or when no specific study is mentioned.
*   **Variant + study** (`get-l2g <variant_id> --study-id <study_id>`): Returns
    L2G predictions only for credible sets from that specific study. Use this
    when the user asks about a specific GWAS study or when you need to narrow
    down the results.

> **Incomplete results warning:** The variant-only mode can return hundreds of
> credible sets. The default `--page-size` is 200, so if the API reports a
> `count` higher than the number of `rows` returned, **you are seeing incomplete
> results**. Always compare `count` to the actual number of rows. If they
> differ, either increase `--page-size` or inform the user that only a subset
> was retrieved.

## Querying by Region

To find studies with variants "nearby" a gene, use
`get-credible-sets-near-target`, which improves upon the base API by performing
a flexible search based on genomic position: `uv run
scripts/query_opentargets.py --output /tmp/results.json
get-credible-sets-near-target ENSG00000156515 --window 500000`

Note that the Open Targets GraphQL schema includes a `regions` parameter for
`credibleSets`, however it performs an exact match against pre-computed region
strings (e.g., `chr10:68769984-69903496`) and there is some missing data. Use
get-credible-sets-near-target as it allows a genomic range overlap search.

This fetches credible sets associated with the target and filters them in Python
based on the variant's genomic position.

## Advanced GraphQL Queries

If you need to query endpoints or fields not exposed by the built-in
subcommands, use the `custom-query` subcommand.

**Before writing a custom query:** Read the reference documentation to
understand the API schema, types, and see example queries. See
[references/OpenTargets_GraphQL_Guide.md](references/OpenTargets_GraphQL_Guide)
for full schema details, endpoints, and examples.

**Example: Finding drugs for a disease**

```bash
uv run scripts/query_opentargets.py custom-query \
  query drugsForDisease($id: String!) {
    disease(efoId: $id) {
      name
      drugAndClinicalCandidates {
        count
        rows {
          maxClinicalStage
          drug {
            id
            name
          }
        }
      }
    }
  }' \
--variables '{"id": "EFO_1001006"}'
--output '/tmp/opentargets_result.json'
```

## Confidence Star Ratings

The Open Targets Platform assigns a **confidence level** to each credible set
based on the fine-mapping method and quality checks. These correspond to star
ratings displayed in the platform UI:

| Stars          | Confidence String (API value)                             |
| -------------- | --------------------------------------------------------- |
| ★★★★ (4 stars) | `SuSiE fine-mapped credible set with in-sample LD`        |
| ★★★ (3 stars)  | `SuSiE fine-mapped credible set with out-of-sample LD`    |
| ★★ (2 stars)   | `PICS fine-mapped credible set extracted from summary     |
:                : statistics`                                               :
| ★ (1 star)     | `PICS fine-mapped credible set based on reported top hit` |
| None           | `Unknown confidence`                                      |

When users ask about "N-star confidence", match their request to the
corresponding string in the `confidence` field of the API response.

## Tips and Common Mistakes

-   **ID Formats**:
    -   Disease IDs are typically MONDO IDs (e.g. `MONDO_0008383` for Rheumatoid
        Arthritis) or EFO IDs (e.g. `EFO_0009460`). Use the `search-disease`
        command to find the correct ID.
    -   Target IDs must be Ensembl IDs (e.g. `ENSG00000169083`), not HGNC
        symbols. If you only have a gene symbol, you may need to map it first
        using a custom GraphQL `search` query.
    -   Variant IDs are formatted as `chromosome_position_ref_alt` (e.g.,
        `1_154426264_C_T`). A `chr` prefix (e.g. `chr1_154426264_C_T`) is
        automatically stripped by the tool.
    -   Study IDs can be GWAS Catalog IDs (e.g. `GCST90204201`) or
        project-specific IDs (e.g. `FINNGEN_R12_RX_CROHN_2NDLINE`).
-   **Truncation**: The tool truncates arrays longer than `--limit` to protect
    the context window. If you see `"_truncated"`, you can run the query again
    with a higher limit if you specifically need more data, but be cautious with
    large limit values. Always use the `--output` flag to save the result to a
    file and avoid terminal output truncation.
-   **Pagination and incomplete results**: The `--page-size` option
    (default: 200) controls how many items are fetched from the API. **Always
    check the `count` field in the response and compare it to the number of
    `rows` actually returned.** If `count` > number of rows, you have incomplete
    data — either increase `--page-size` to fetch more, or inform the user that
    only a partial result set was returned. This is especially important for
    `get-l2g` without `--study-id`, which can return hundreds of credible sets.</SKILL>
</ADDITIONAL_METADATA>

462      rc = py.AddVariable(&vf_timeID, 'ALIAS', 'ds');
463      rc = py.AddVariable(CHRONOS_URL);
464      rc = py.AddVariable(_LEAD_); /*pass the predefined variable to TF*/
465      rc = py.AddVariable(NUM_SAMPLES);
466      rc = py.AddVariable(TEMPERATURE);
467      rc = py.AddVariable(TOP_K);
468      rc = py.AddVariable(TOP_P);
469      rc = py.AddVariable(ffm_fcst,"READONLY","NO","ARRAYRESIZE","YES","ALIAS",'PREDICT');
470  	rc = py.AddVariable(ffm_lfcst,"READONLY","NO","ARRAYRESIZE","YES","ALIAS",'LOWER');
471  	rc = py.AddVariable(ffm_ufcst,"READONLY","NO","ARRAYRESIZE","YES","ALIAS",'UPPER');
472  	rc = py.AddVariable(ffm_err,"READONLY","NO","ARRAYRESIZE","YES","ALIAS",'ERR');
7                                                          The SAS System                       Tuesday, August 11, 2026 09:19:00 AM

473  	rc = py.AddVariable(ffm_stderr,"READONLY","NO","ARRAYRESIZE","YES","ALIAS",'STDERR');
474       * rc = py.AddEnvVariable('_TKMBPY_DEBUG_FILES_PATH', &log_folder);
475  
476  	/* Packages needed for processing the call */
477      rc = py.pushCodeLine("import json");
478      rc = py.pushCodeLine("import math");
479      rc = py.pushCodeLine("import requests as req");
480      rc = py.pushCodeLine("import numpy as np");
481  	
482  	/* Import the variables set outside of the python code */
483      rc = py.pushCodeLine("url = CHRONOS_URL");
484      rc = py.pushCodeLine("prediction_length = int(_LEAD_)");
485      rc = py.pushCodeLine("num_samples = int(NUM_SAMPLES)");
486      rc = py.pushCodeLine("temperature = float(TEMPERATURE)");
487      rc = py.pushCodeLine("top_k = int(TOP_K)");
488      rc = py.pushCodeLine("top_p = float(TOP_P)");
489  
490      /****************************************************************** */
491      /* First, to get some fit statistics for the model, a prediction    */
492      /* is called on the last _LEAD_ points of the existing data.        */
493      /* Afterwards, it is run for the time window after the last given.  */
494      /****************************************************************** */
495  
496      /* Set the options for the model API call */
497      rc = py.pushCodeLine("payload = json.dumps({");
498      rc = py.pushCodeLine("	'prediction_length': prediction_length,");
499      rc = py.pushCodeLine("	'num_samples': num_samples,");
500      rc = py.pushCodeLine("	'temperature': temperature,");
501      rc = py.pushCodeLine("	'top_k': top_k,");
502      rc = py.pushCodeLine("	'top_p': top_p,");
503      rc = py.pushCodeLine("	'data': TARGET.tolist()[:-prediction_length]");
504      rc = py.pushCodeLine("})");
505      rc = py.pushCodeLine("headers = {'Content-Type': 'application/json'}");
506  
507      /* The call will return a string with 3 (Median Forecast and limits of the 80% prediction interval) */
508  	/* by X values, where X is the prediction length. The values are separated by commas.               */
509      rc = py.pushCodeLine("resp = req.post(url, headers=headers, data=payload, verify=False)");
510  
511  	/* Process the resulting values into workable objects */
512      rc = py.pushCodeLine("forecast_validation = resp.text.splitlines()");
513      rc = py.pushCodeLine("forecast_validation_values = np.genfromtxt(forecast_validation, delimiter=',', skip_header=1)");
514  
515      /* Repeat the above steps for the forecast horizon. */
516      rc = py.pushCodeLine("payload = json.dumps({");
517      rc = py.pushCodeLine("	'prediction_length': prediction_length,");
518      rc = py.pushCodeLine("	'num_samples': num_samples,");
519      rc = py.pushCodeLine("	'temperature': temperature,");
520      rc = py.pushCodeLine("	'top_k': top_k,");
521      rc = py.pushCodeLine("	'top_p': top_p,");
522      rc = py.pushCodeLine("	'data': TARGET.tolist()");
523      rc = py.pushCodeLine("})");
524      rc = py.pushCodeLine("headers = {'Content-Type': 'application/json'}");
525  
526      rc = py.pushCodeLine("resp = req.post(url, headers=headers, data=payload, verify=False)");
527  
528      rc = py.pushCodeLine("forecast = resp.text.splitlines()");
529      rc = py.pushCodeLine("forecast_header = forecast[0].split(',')");
530      rc = py.pushCodeLine("forecast_values = np.genfromtxt(forecast, delimiter=',', skip_header=1)");
8                                                          The SAS System                       Tuesday, August 11, 2026 09:19:00 AM

531  
532  	/* Generate an empty numpy ndarray so that the predicted values are appended at the right index */
533      rc = py.pushCodeLine("prediction = np.empty(TARGET.shape[0] - (2 * prediction_length))");
534      rc = py.pushCodeLine("prediction[:] = np.nan");
535  
536  	/* Repeat for the lower and upper prediction interval borders and the standard error */
537      rc = py.pushCodeLine("lower = np.empty(TARGET.shape[0] - (2 * prediction_length))");
538      rc = py.pushCodeLine("lower[:] = np.nan");
539  	rc = py.pushCodeLine("upper = np.empty(TARGET.shape[0] - (2 * prediction_length))");
540      rc = py.pushCodeLine("upper[:] = np.nan");
541  
542  
543      rc = py.pushCodeLine("prediction = np.concatenate((prediction, forecast_validation_values[:,
543! forecast_header.index('median')]))");
544      rc = py.pushCodeLine("prediction = np.concatenate((prediction, forecast_values[:, forecast_header.index('median')]))");
545  
546  
547      rc = py.pushCodeLine("lower = np.concatenate((lower, forecast_validation_values[:, forecast_header.index('low')]))");
548      rc = py.pushCodeLine("lower = np.concatenate((lower, forecast_values[:, forecast_header.index('low')]))");
549  	
550  
551      rc = py.pushCodeLine("upper = np.concatenate((upper, forecast_validation_values[:, forecast_header.index('high')]))");
552      rc = py.pushCodeLine("upper = np.concatenate((upper, forecast_values[:, forecast_header.index('high')]))");
553  
554      /* ALso create arrays for the prediction error and standard error of the interval */
555      rc = py.pushCodeLine("err = TARGET - prediction");
556  
557  	rc = py.pushCodeLine("interval_width = upper - lower");
558  	rc = py.pushCodeLine("z_score = 1.28");
559  	rc = py.pushCodeLine("stderr = interval_width / (2 * z_score)");
560  
561      /* Map the arrays created in the python code to the SAS Series */
562  	rc = py.pushCodeLine("PREDICT = prediction");
563  	rc = py.pushCodeLine("LOWER = lower");
564  	rc = py.pushCodeLine("UPPER = upper");
565  	rc = py.pushCodeLine("ERR = err");
566  	rc = py.pushCodeLine("STDERR = stderr");
567  
568      rc = py.Run();
569  
570  	declare object pylog(OUTEXTLOG);
571  	rc = pylog.Collect(py, 'EXECUTION');
572  
573  	declare object outvarstatus(OUTEXTVARSTATUS);
574  	rc = outvarstatus.Collect(py);
575  
576  	declare object pyExmSpec(EXMSPEC);
577  	rc = pyExmSpec.open();
578  	rc = pyExmSpec.setOption('METHOD', 'PERFECT');
579  	rc = pyExmSpec.setOption('NLAGPCT', 0);
580  	rc = pyExmSpec.setOption('PREDICT', 'ffm_fcst');
581  	rc = pyExmSpec.setOption('LOWER', 'ffm_lfcst');
582  	rc = pyExmSpec.setOption('UPPER', 'ffm_ufcst');
583  	rc = pyExmSpec.setOption('STDERR', 'ffm_stderr');
584  	rc = pyExmSpec.close();
585  
586      declare object tsm(tsm);
587

591  	rc = tsm.AddExternal(ffm_lfcst, 'LOWER');
592  	rc = tsm.AddExternal(ffm_ufcst, 'UPPER');
593      rc = tsm.AddExternal(ffm_err, 'ERROR');

338  %macro __validateBackendArgs;
339      %global preProcessErrorStatus;
340      %if "&vf_classification" eq "pluggable_hierarchical" %then %do;
341          proc cas;
342              %include "&sas_root_location/misc/codegenscrpt/source/casl/hfp_args_comparison.casl";
343              errorStatus = 0;
344              args = json2casl(symget('_backendArgs'));
345              if args._forecastTask ne "DIAGNOSE" then do;
346                  argsLast = json2casl(symget('_backendArgsLast'));
347                  if dim(argsLast) ne 0 then do;
348                      argsComp = hfp_args_comparison(args, argsLast);
349                      errorStatus = argsComp.errorStatus;
350                      if argsComp.errorStatus eq 0 then symputx('_forecastTask', argsComp.forecastTask);
351                  end;
352                  else do;
353                      print (error) "The DIAGNOSE task has never been executed and should be successfully executed at least once
353! before any other forecast task can be enabled.";
354                      errorStatus = 1;
355                  end;
356              end;
357              symputx('preProcessErrorStatus', errorStatus);
358          quit;
359      %end;
360      %else %if "&vf_classification" eq "pluggable_auto" %then %do;

366                  args._forecastTask = "DIAGNOSE";

368              if args._forecastTask ne "DIAGNOSE" then do;

453      NUM_SAMPLES = &_num_samples.;               /* increases performance and runtime    */
454      TEMPERATURE = &_temperature.;               /* token generation temperature         */
455      TOP_K = &_top_k.;                           /* token generation Top-K               */
456      TOP_P = &_top_p.;                           /* token generation Top-p               */

459      rc = py.Initialize();
460  
461  	rc = py.AddVariable(&vf_depVar,'ALIAS','TARGET') ;
462      rc = py.AddVariable(&vf_timeID, 'ALIAS', 'ds');
463      rc = py.AddVariable(CHRONOS_URL);
464      rc = py.AddVariable(_LEAD_); /*pass the predefined variable to TF*/
465      rc = py.AddVariable(NUM_SAMPLES);
466      rc = py.AddVariable(TEMPERATURE);
467      rc = py.AddVariable(TOP_K);
468      rc = py.AddVariable(TOP_P);
469      rc = py.AddVariable(ffm_fcst,"READONLY","NO","ARRAYRESIZE","YES","ALIAS",'PREDICT');
470  	rc = py.AddVariable(ffm_lfcst,"READONLY","NO","ARRAYRESIZE","YES","ALIAS",'LOWER');
471  	rc = py.AddVariable(ffm_ufcst,"READONLY","NO","ARRAYRESIZE","YES","ALIAS",'UPPER');
472  	rc = py.AddVariable(ffm_err,"READONLY","NO","ARRAYRESIZE","YES","ALIAS",'ERR');
7                                                          The SAS System                       Tuesday, August 11, 2026 09:24:00 AM

473  	rc = py.AddVariable(ffm_stderr,"READONLY","NO","ARRAYRESIZE","YES","ALIAS",'STDERR');
474       * rc = py.AddEnvVariable('_TKMBPY_DEBUG_FILES_PATH', &log_folder);
475  
476  	/* Packages needed for processing the call */
477      rc = py.pushCodeLine("import math");
478      rc = py.pushCodeLine("import numpy as np");
479      rc = py.pushCodeLine("import torch");
480      rc = py.pushCodeLine("from chronos import ChronosPipeline");
481  	
482  	/* Import the variables set outside of the python code */
483      rc = py.pushCodeLine("prediction_length = int(_LEAD_)");
484      rc = py.pushCodeLine("num_samples = int(NUM_SAMPLES)");
485      rc = py.pushCodeLine("temperature = float(TEMPERATURE)");
486      rc = py.pushCodeLine("top_k = int(TOP_K)");
487      rc = py.pushCodeLine("top_p = float(TOP_P)");
488  
489      /****************************************************************** */
490      /* Run Chronos natively on the local compute node without an API    */
491      /****************************************************************** */
492  
493      rc = py.pushCodeLine("device = 'cuda' if torch.cuda.is_available() else 'cpu'");
494      rc = py.pushCodeLine("pipeline = ChronosPipeline.from_pretrained(");
495      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    'amazon/chronos-t5-small',");
496      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    device_map=device,");
497      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    torch_dtype=torch.bfloat16");
498      rc = py.pushCodeLine(")");
499  
500      /* First, to get some fit statistics for the model, a prediction    */
501      /* is called on the last _LEAD_ points of the existing data.        */
502  
503      rc = py.pushCodeLine("context_validation = torch.tensor(TARGET.tolist()[:-prediction_length])");
504      rc = py.pushCodeLine("val_forecast = pipeline.predict(");
505      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    context=context_validation,");
506      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    prediction_length=prediction_length,");
507      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    num_samples=num_samples,");
508      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    temperature=temperature,");
509      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    top_k=top_k,");
510      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    top_p=top_p");
511      rc = py.pushCodeLine(")");
512      rc = py.pushCodeLine("val_median = np.quantile(val_forecast[0].numpy(), 0.5, axis=0)");
513      rc = py.pushCodeLine("val_low = np.quantile(val_forecast[0].numpy(), 0.1, axis=0)");
514      rc = py.pushCodeLine("val_high = np.quantile(val_forecast[0].numpy(), 0.9, axis=0)");
515  
516      /* Repeat the above steps for the forecast horizon. */
517  
518      rc = py.pushCodeLine("context_full = torch.tensor(TARGET.tolist())");
519      rc = py.pushCodeLine("test_forecast = pipeline.predict(");
520      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    context=context_full,");
521      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    prediction_length=prediction_length,");
522      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    num_samples=num_samples,");
523      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    temperature=temperature,");
524      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    top_k=top_k,");
525      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    top_p=top_p");
526      rc = py.pushCodeLine(")");
527      rc = py.pushCodeLine("test_median = np.quantile(test_forecast[0].numpy(), 0.5, axis=0)");
528      rc = py.pushCodeLine("test_low = np.quantile(test_forecast[0].numpy(), 0.1, axis=0)");
529      rc = py.pushCodeLine("test_high = np.quantile(test_forecast[0].numpy(), 0.9, axis=0)");
530  
8                                                          The SAS System                       Tuesday, August 11, 2026 09:24:00 AM

531  	/* Generate an empty numpy ndarray so that the predicted values are appended at the right index */
532      rc = py.pushCodeLine("prediction = np.empty(TARGET.shape[0] - (2 * prediction_length))");
533      rc = py.pushCodeLine("prediction[:] = np.nan");
534  	/* Repeat for the lower and upper prediction interval borders and the standard error */
535      rc = py.pushCodeLine("lower = np.empty(TARGET.shape[0] - (2 * prediction_length))");
536      rc = py.pushCodeLine("lower[:] = np.nan");
537  	rc = py.pushCodeLine("upper = np.empty(TARGET.shape[0] - (2 * prediction_length))");
538      rc = py.pushCodeLine("upper[:] = np.nan");
539  
540      rc = py.pushCodeLine("prediction = np.concatenate((prediction, val_median))");
541      rc = py.pushCodeLine("prediction = np.concatenate((prediction, test_median))");
542  
543      rc = py.pushCodeLine("lower = np.concatenate((lower, val_low))");
544      rc = py.pushCodeLine("lower = np.concatenate((lower, test_low))");
545  	
546      rc = py.pushCodeLine("upper = np.concatenate((upper, val_high))");
547      rc = py.pushCodeLine("upper = np.concatenate((upper, test_high))");
548  
549      /* ALso create arrays for the prediction error and standard error of the interval */
550      rc = py.pushCodeLine("err = TARGET - prediction");
551  
552  	rc = py.pushCodeLine("interval_width = upper - lower");
553  	rc = py.pushCodeLine("z_score = 1.28");
554  	rc = py.pushCodeLine("stderr = interval_width / (2 * z_score)");
555  
556      /* Map the arrays created in the python code to the SAS Series */
557  	rc = py.pushCodeLine("PREDICT = prediction");
558  	rc = py.pushCodeLine("LOWER = lower");
559  	rc = py.pushCodeLine("UPPER = upper");
560  	rc = py.pushCodeLine("ERR = err");
561  	rc = py.pushCodeLine("STDERR = stderr");
562  
563      rc = py.Run();
564  
565  	declare object pylog(OUTEXTLOG);
566  	rc = pylog.Collect(py, 'EXECUTION');
567  
568  	declare object outvarstatus(OUTEXTVARSTATUS);
569  	rc = outvarstatus.Collect(py);
570  
571  	declare object pyExmSpec(EXMSPEC);
572  	rc = pyExmSpec.open();
573  	rc = pyExmSpec.setOption('METHOD', 'PERFECT');
574  	rc = pyExmSpec.setOption('NLAGPCT', 0);
575  	rc = pyExmSpec.setOption('PREDICT', 'ffm_fcst');
576  	rc = pyExmSpec.setOption('LOWER', 'ffm_lfcst');
577  	rc = pyExmSpec.setOption('UPPER', 'ffm_ufcst');
578  	rc = pyExmSpec.setOption('STDERR', 'ffm_stderr');
579  	rc = pyExmSpec.close();
580

583  	
584      rc = tsm.Initialize(pyExmSpec);
585      rc = tsm.AddExternal(ffm_fcst, 'PREDICT');
586  	rc = tsm.AddExternal(ffm_lfcst, 'LOWER');
587  	rc = tsm.AddExternal(ffm_ufcst, 'UPPER');

MPRINT(VF_WRAPPER):   rc = py.pushCodeLine("val_forecast = pipeline.predict(");

MPRINT(VF_WRAPPER):   rc = py.pushCodeLine(" prediction_length=prediction_length,");
MPRINT(VF_WRAPPER):   rc = py.pushCodeLine(" num_samples=num_samples,");

MPRINT(VF_WRAPPER):   rc = py.pushCodeLine(" num_samples=num_samples,");

MPRINT(VF_WRAPPER):   rc = py.pushCodeLine(" top_k=top_k,");

MPRINT(VF_WRAPPER):   rc = py.pushCodeLine(")");

453      NUM_SAMPLES = &_num_samples.;               /* increases performance and runtime    */
454      TEMPERATURE = &_temperature.;               /* token generation temperature         */
455      TOP_K = &_top_k.;                           /* token generation Top-K               */

458      declare object py(PYTHON3);
459      rc = py.Initialize();
460  
461  	rc = py.AddVariable(&vf_depVar,'ALIAS','TARGET') ;
462      rc = py.AddVariable(&vf_timeID, 'ALIAS', 'ds');
463      rc = py.AddVariable(CHRONOS_URL);
464      rc = py.AddVariable(_LEAD_); /*pass the predefined variable to TF*/
465      rc = py.AddVariable(NUM_SAMPLES);
466      rc = py.AddVariable(TEMPERATURE);
467      rc = py.AddVariable(TOP_K);
468      rc = py.AddVariable(TOP_P);
469      rc = py.AddVariable(ffm_fcst,"READONLY","NO","ARRAYRESIZE","YES","ALIAS",'PREDICT');
470  	rc = py.AddVariable(ffm_lfcst,"READONLY","NO","ARRAYRESIZE","YES","ALIAS",'LOWER');
471  	rc = py.AddVariable(ffm_ufcst,"READONLY","NO","ARRAYRESIZE","YES","ALIAS",'UPPER');
472  	rc = py.AddVariable(ffm_err,"READONLY","NO","ARRAYRESIZE","YES","ALIAS",'ERR');
7                                                          The SAS System                       Tuesday, August 11, 2026 10:02:00 AM

473  	rc = py.AddVariable(ffm_stderr,"READONLY","NO","ARRAYRESIZE","YES","ALIAS",'STDERR');
474       * rc = py.AddEnvVariable('_TKMBPY_DEBUG_FILES_PATH', &log_folder);
475  
476  	/* Packages needed for processing the call */
477      rc = py.pushCodeLine("try:");
478      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    import math");
479      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    import numpy as np");
480      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    import torch");
481      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    from chronos import ChronosPipeline");
482      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    ");
483      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    prediction_length = int(_LEAD_)");
484      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    num_samples = int(NUM_SAMPLES)");
485      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    temperature = float(TEMPERATURE)");
486      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    top_k = int(TOP_K)");
487      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    top_p = float(TOP_P)");
488      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    target_list = TARGET.tolist()");
489      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    N = len(target_list)");
490      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    ");
491      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    if N <= prediction_length * 2:");
492      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        raise ValueError(f'Time series too short for validation: len={N},
492! lead={prediction_length}')");
493      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    ");
494      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    device = 'cuda' if torch.cuda.is_available() else 'cpu'");
495      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    pipeline = ChronosPipeline.from_pretrained(");
496      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        'amazon/chronos-t5-small',");
497      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        device_map=device,");
498      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        torch_dtype=torch.bfloat16");
499      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    )");
500      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    ");
501      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    context_validation = torch.tensor(target_list[:-prediction_length])");
502      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    val_forecast = pipeline.predict(");
503      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        context=context_validation,");
504      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        prediction_length=prediction_length,");
505      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        num_samples=num_samples,");
506      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        temperature=temperature,");
507      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        top_k=top_k,");
508      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        top_p=top_p");
509      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    )");
510      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    val_median = np.quantile(val_forecast[0].numpy(), 0.5, axis=0)");
511      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    val_low = np.quantile(val_forecast[0].numpy(), 0.1, axis=0)");
512      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    val_high = np.quantile(val_forecast[0].numpy(), 0.9, axis=0)");
513      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    ");
514      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    context_full = torch.tensor(target_list)");
515      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    test_forecast = pipeline.predict(");
516      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        context=context_full,");
517      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        prediction_length=prediction_length,");
518      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        num_samples=num_samples,");
519      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        temperature=temperature,");
520      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        top_k=top_k,");
521      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        top_p=top_p");
522      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    )");
523      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    test_median = np.quantile(test_forecast[0].numpy(), 0.5, axis=0)");
524      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    test_low = np.quantile(test_forecast[0].numpy(), 0.1, axis=0)");
525      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    test_high = np.quantile(test_forecast[0].numpy(), 0.9, axis=0)");
526      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    ");
527      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    prediction = np.empty(N - (2 * prediction_length))");
528      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    prediction[:] = np.nan");
529      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    lower = np.empty(N - (2 * prediction_length))");
8                                                          The SAS System                       Tuesday, August 11, 2026 10:02:00 AM

530      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    lower[:] = np.nan");
531      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    upper = np.empty(N - (2 * prediction_length))");
532      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    upper[:] = np.nan");
533      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    ");
534      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    prediction = np.concatenate((prediction, val_median, test_median))");
535      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    lower = np.concatenate((lower, val_low, test_low))");
536      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    upper = np.concatenate((upper, val_high, test_high))");
537      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    ");
538      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    err = TARGET - prediction");
539      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    interval_width = upper - lower");
540      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    stderr = interval_width / (2 * 1.28)");
541      rc = py.pushCodeLine("except Exception as e:");
542      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    import traceback, numpy as np");
543      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    with open('/tmp/chronos_py_err.log', 'w') as f:");
544      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        f.write(traceback.format_exc())");
545      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    N = len(TARGET.tolist())");
546      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    prediction = np.empty(N)");
547      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    prediction[:] = np.nan");
548      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    lower, upper, err, stderr = prediction.copy(), prediction.copy(), prediction.copy(),
548! prediction.copy()");
549      rc = py.pushCodeLine("");
550      rc = py.pushCodeLine("PREDICT = prediction");
551      rc = py.pushCodeLine("LOWER = lower");
552      rc = py.pushCodeLine("UPPER = upper");
553      rc = py.pushCodeLine("ERR = err");
554      rc = py.pushCodeLine("STDERR = stderr");
555  
556      rc = py.Run();

MPRINT(VF_WRAPPER):   rc = py.pushCodeLine(" interval_width = upper - lower");
MPRINT(VF_WRAPPER):   rc = py.pushCodeLine(" stderr = interval_width / (2 * 1.28)");
MPRINT(VF_WRAPPER):   rc = py.pushCodeLine("except Exception as e:");
MPRINT(VF_WRAPPER):   rc = py.pushCodeLine(" import traceback, numpy as np");
MPRINT(VF_WRAPPER):   rc = py.pushCodeLine(" with open('/tmp/chronos_py_err.log', 'w') as f:");

MPRINT(VF_WRAPPER):   rc = py.pushCodeLine(" N = len(TARGET.tolist())");
MPRINT(VF_WRAPPER):   rc = py.pushCodeLine(" prediction = np.empty(N)");
MPRINT(VF_WRAPPER):   rc = py.pushCodeLine(" prediction[:] = np.nan");
MPRINT(VF_WRAPPER):   rc = py.pushCodeLine(" lower, upper, err, stderr = prediction.copy(), prediction.copy(), prediction.copy(), 
prediction.copy()");

MPRINT(VF_WRAPPER):   rc = py.pushCodeLine("UPPER = upper");

464      rc = py.AddVariable(_LEAD_); /*pass the predefined variable to TF*/
465      rc = py.AddVariable(NUM_SAMPLES);
466      rc = py.AddVariable(TEMPERATURE);
467      rc = py.AddVariable(TOP_K);
468      rc = py.AddVariable(TOP_P);
469      rc = py.AddVariable(ffm_fcst,"READONLY","NO","ARRAYRESIZE","YES","ALIAS",'PREDICT');
470  	rc = py.AddVariable(ffm_lfcst,"READONLY","NO","ARRAYRESIZE","YES","ALIAS",'LOWER');
471  	rc = py.AddVariable(ffm_ufcst,"READONLY","NO","ARRAYRESIZE","YES","ALIAS",'UPPER');
472  	rc = py.AddVariable(ffm_err,"READONLY","NO","ARRAYRESIZE","YES","ALIAS",'ERR');
7                                                          The SAS System                       Tuesday, August 11, 2026 10:14:00 AM

473  	rc = py.AddVariable(ffm_stderr,"READONLY","NO","ARRAYRESIZE","YES","ALIAS",'STDERR');
474       * rc = py.AddEnvVariable('_TKMBPY_DEBUG_FILES_PATH', &log_folder);
475  
476  	/* Packages needed for processing the call */
477      rc = py.pushCodeLine("try:");
478      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    import math");
479      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    import numpy as np");
480      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    import torch");
481      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    from chronos import ChronosPipeline");
482      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    ");
483      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    prediction_length = int(_LEAD_)");
484      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    num_samples = int(NUM_SAMPLES)");
485      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    temperature = float(TEMPERATURE)");
486      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    top_k = int(TOP_K)");
487      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    top_p = float(TOP_P)");
488      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    N = len(TARGET.tolist())");
489      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    target_list_valid = [x for x in TARGET.tolist() if not math.isnan(x)]");
490      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    ");
491      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    if len(target_list_valid) <= prediction_length * 2:");
492      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        raise ValueError(f'Time series too short for validation: len={len(target_list_valid)},
492! lead={prediction_length}')");
493      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    ");
494      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    device = 'cuda' if torch.cuda.is_available() else 'cpu'");
495      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    pipeline = ChronosPipeline.from_pretrained(");
496      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        'amazon/chronos-t5-small',");
497      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        device_map=device,");
498      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        torch_dtype=torch.bfloat16");
499      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    )");
500      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    ");
501      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    context_validation = torch.tensor(target_list_valid[:-prediction_length])");
502      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    val_forecast = pipeline.predict(");
503      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        context=context_validation,");
504      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        prediction_length=prediction_length,");
505      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        num_samples=num_samples,");
506      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        temperature=temperature,");
507      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        top_k=top_k,");
508      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        top_p=top_p");
509      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    )");
510      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    val_median = np.quantile(val_forecast[0].numpy(), 0.5, axis=0)");
511      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    val_low = np.quantile(val_forecast[0].numpy(), 0.1, axis=0)");
512      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    val_high = np.quantile(val_forecast[0].numpy(), 0.9, axis=0)");
513      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    ");
514      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    context_full = torch.tensor(target_list_valid)");
515      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    test_forecast = pipeline.predict(");
516      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        context=context_full,");
517      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        prediction_length=prediction_length,");
518      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        num_samples=num_samples,");
519      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        temperature=temperature,");
520      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        top_k=top_k,");
521      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        top_p=top_p");
522      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    )");
523      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    test_median = np.quantile(test_forecast[0].numpy(), 0.5, axis=0)");
524      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    test_low = np.quantile(test_forecast[0].numpy(), 0.1, axis=0)");
525      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    test_high = np.quantile(test_forecast[0].numpy(), 0.9, axis=0)");
526      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    ");
527      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    prediction = np.empty(N - (2 * prediction_length))");
528      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    prediction[:] = np.nan");
529      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    lower = np.empty(N - (2 * prediction_length))");
8                                                          The SAS System                       Tuesday, August 11, 2026 10:14:00 AM

530      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    lower[:] = np.nan");
531      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    upper = np.empty(N - (2 * prediction_length))");
532      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    upper[:] = np.nan");
533      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    ");
534      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    prediction = np.concatenate((prediction, val_median, test_median))");
535      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    lower = np.concatenate((lower, val_low, test_low))");
536      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    upper = np.concatenate((upper, val_high, test_high))");
537      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    ");
538      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    err = TARGET - prediction");
539      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    interval_width = upper - lower");
540      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    stderr = interval_width / (2 * 1.28)");
541      rc = py.pushCodeLine("except Exception as e:");
542      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    import traceback, numpy as np");
543      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    with open('/tmp/chronos_py_err.log', 'w') as f:");
544      rc = py.pushCodeLine("        f.write(traceback.format_exc())");
545      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    N = len(TARGET.tolist())");
546      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    prediction = np.empty(N)");
547      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    prediction[:] = np.nan");
548      rc = py.pushCodeLine("    lower, upper, err, stderr = prediction.copy(), prediction.copy(), prediction.copy(),
548! prediction.copy()");
549      rc = py.pushCodeLine("");
550      rc = py.pushCodeLine("PREDICT = prediction");
551      rc = py.pushCodeLine("LOWER = lower");
552      rc = py.pushCodeLine("UPPER = upper");
553      rc = py.pushCodeLine("ERR = err");
554      rc = py.pushCodeLine("STDERR = stderr");
555  
556      rc = py.Run();
